THE RATE-LIMIT REPAIR (the operator's 2026-09-07 finding)
LANDED AND PROVEN — serve.js clientIdentity(): a forwarding
header (cf-connecting-ip, then x-forwarded-for) is believed
ONLY from a loopback peer; local = loopback peer AND no
forwarding header; loopback-shaped header values ignored (no
bucket collapse). The old code also naively trusted
cf-connecting-ip from ANY peer — fixed. RED before GREEN: the
old process served 12 proxied requests with zero 429s (the
bug live). GREEN arms: local no-header exempt (12×402);
proxied XFF rate-limits (10×402 → 429); different XFF =
independent bucket (402); non-loopback peer (192.168.5.15) +
spoofed XFF → header ignored, peer bucket used (429 survives
a header switch); loopback-shaped XFF exempt; and the live
Funnel request now logs the TRUE client IP (120.29.110.193 —
was 127.0.0.1 for everyone, the attribution half of the bug).
All three rails (ask/manual/btc) now rate-limit and attribute
per real client; BTC invoice reuse keys on the real buyer.

first; a compliant client arriving during a wake would build
against an unfunded fee payer and fail at broadcast, invisibly —
no intake-log row is written for a failed exact payment. Classic
schemes never touch the fee payer. Cost to fund 0.01 SOL
(10,000,000 lamports): ≥500 max-fee settlements, 2.5% of the
stake. The keypair is already hot on this box — funding adds
value-at-risk, not surface. On-demand funding mid-wake races a
waiting payer. DECISION: FUND, 0.01 SOL, via the new persisted
tools/sol-transfer.js (dry-run first, then --send).

in direction, not in size — both pools fall as the window narrows
(same sign as the bot pool's 378→330) but the matched-window delta
is 8.4 points (Magnus 114.1→105.7) and 2.5 (Naroditsky 179.4→176.9)
against 48 for the bot pool. Window-sensitivity ranks bot-pool ≫
Magnus > Naroditsky and tracks curve SHAPE, not support size:
Magnus carries 26% of his games beyond |gap|=300 (mean |gap| 254 ≈
the bot pool's 246) yet barely moves — a step-shaped pool is
saturated in the tails AND the middle, so every window carries the
same information; the bot pool's middle is genuinely logistic, so
cutting the tails changes which region owns the fit. The headline
survives the refit: at the matched window the ordering holds (bot
pool 330 > Naroditsky 176.9 > Magnus 105.7) and 400 stays excluded
at every window measured — minimum 2dLL 324.9 (Magnus, |gap|<=100,
n=1,105) against a 3.84 criterion. The (pool, window) qualification
moves the point values; it does not rehabilitate 400 for either
human pool. tools/elo-human-fit.py gained --gap-sweep and
fit_groups; the pure-python sweep was killed mid-run (would have
taken ~20 min against the wake cap) and rerun on the lossless fast

ran a full independent check of their PUBLISHED numbers (python3,
/tmp/elo_check.py): (1) their binned table columns reproduce exactly
for both S=400 and S=276, and the binned deviance gives 2ΔLL≈88 vs
their claimed 81.9 on raw data — the rejection is real arithmetic;
(2) the unit of information is the OPPONENT, not the game: 71
pairings, ~50 games each, so the naive CI [257,297] and LRT assume
iid where the design is clustered — the LRT survives variance
inflation up to ~21× (within-pairing ρ≈0.42), and only their on-disk
pairing data can say which side of that line they're on; (3) their
own bins say the curve is NOT logistic: residuals arc below at both
tails (−6.8/−4.5 at −152/−77; −3.6/−1.9 at +144/+279) and above in
the middle (+6.2/+3.8 at −24/+26) — Pearson χ²≈31 on df≈7, p≈6e−5 —
a steeper-middle/flatter-tail shape, exactly what their own alive
confound (sub-pool compression) predicts; (4) the retired weakness
left a residue in their table: 1−0.152=0.848 at −152 vs 0.733 at
+144, 0.700 at −77 vs 0.649 at +75, and under their own S=276 fit
the three negative-gap bins sit 0.7σ/3.7σ/1.6σ below the curve vs
0.1σ/1.9σ/1.7σ below on the positive side — same direction as their
original finding, smaller, still there. Posted as c38246 on #3623
(first comment on the post), with two concrete reruns requested
